About AI discoveries in 2026

This selection contains 1448 catalog entries dated 1 Jan 2026 to 24 Nov 2026. 1447 include structured evidence annotations describing reported tests, the AI contribution and remaining limits. Missing annotations mean those details have not been structured here, not that a study lacks evidence.

Dates follow the catalog publication policy. Coverage is selected and incomplete; entries can overlap or describe several results. Counts do not measure independent discoveries, clinical benefit or worldwide research output. Read the selection and evidence policy or compare catalog coverage by year and field.

24 Nov 2026Fujian Medical University Union Hospital

Survival learning prioritizes cohort-specific NFKB1 associations in colorectal cancer

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A study used penalized Cox models and CoxBoost to prioritize NFKB1 for retrospective colorectal-cancer survival analysis. Higher expression was associated with favorable survival in selected external cohorts, but the adjusted association was not significant in TCGA-COAD. Model discrimination was modest: the best overall mean time-dependent AUC was 0.650. A separate spatial analysis used one tissue specimen and does not establish macrophage-specific causality. NFKB1 remains an exploratory association, without a validated clinical cutoff or demonstrated treatment benefit.

Technology: LASSO-Cox, ridge/elastic-net Cox and CoxBoost survival learning

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Published article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Learned survival models prioritize genes for prognostic association analysis; language assistants did not conduct scientific analysis.
Test and result
Existing TCGA development data and separate GEO cohort survival associations; heterogeneous endpoints and moderate discrimination.
Limitation / still unknown
Association is not uniformly significant across cohorts or causal; one spatial specimen and exploratory protein-image comparisons constrain interpretation. Drug-response estimates are predictions.

23 Nov 2026University of Arkansas for Medical Sciences

AlphaFold3 modeling supports a functional COG4–Syntaxin 5 interface

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A preprint abstract reports that AlphaFold3 modeling placed COG4 against a Syntaxin5 loop/linker region, supporting a proposed interface in Golgi membrane trafficking. Targeted substitutions, co-precipitation and cell-based rescue assays linked that interface to protein glycosylation and Golgi organization. This is an abstract-scoped source check: full Methods, sample sizes and detailed controls were inaccessible, and no organism-level or clinical effect is established.

Technology: AlphaFold 3

Family AlphaFold: profile · models page

Editorial relevance 60 biology Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
AlphaFold3 predicted a COG4–Syntaxin5 contact arrangement; the reported cell perturbations test the functional interface described by that model.
Test and result
The primary abstract reports targeted interface substitutions, co-precipitation, rescue, glycosylation, localization and Golgi-morphology assays in cells.
Limitation / still unknown
Full Methods, sample sizes and detailed experimental controls were not assessed because primary full-text routes were inaccessible. The reviewed material does not establish organism-level or clinical effects.

23 Nov 2026Institute of Bioinformatics, Bangalore; Indian Council of Medical Research; University of Delhi

Transcriptomic classifier shows limited cross-dataset Long COVID discrimination

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A bioRxiv preprint from the Institute of Bioinformatics and collaborators analyzes six RNA-sequencing datasets from Long COVID and recovered patients. Its primary abstract reports mean AUC0.664 for an RBF support-vector classifier across six leave-one-batch-out folds. Gene hypotheses were derived from three selected higher-performing folds and then used for pathway and interaction analyses; these do not establish causal mechanisms, effective drugs or clinical utility. This review covers the primary abstract and metadata; full-text HTML and PDF access were blocked.

Technology: RBF-kernel support vector classifier; SHAP

Editorial relevance 40 medicine PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The learned RBF support-vector classifier distinguishes Long COVID from recovered-patient transcriptomic profiles across held-out batches; SHAP nominates features for further analyses.
Test and result
Retrospective leave-one-batch-out discrimination across six transcriptomic datasets, mean AUC0.664; SHAP-derived features from three selected folds support hypothesis generation.
Limitation / still unknown
Full methods, patient counts, leakage controls and batch-specific performance were not assessed because full-text routes were inaccessible. Experimental mechanisms, drug benefit and clinical utility are not established.

23 Nov 2026Northwestern University; Shirley Ryan AbilityLab; Karolinska Institutet; University of California, San Diego; McGill University

Cross-species classifier identifies a human dopaminergic subtype depleted in Parkinson’s disease

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A hierarchical mixture-of-experts classifier transferred mouse dopaminergic-neuron labels to human single-nucleus datasets, identifying an Anxa1-associated population. The preprint reports this population was more depleted in Parkinson’s disease than comparator subtypes in a postmortem analysis of eight control and seven Parkinson’s donors. The model uses logistic-regression experts trained on a mouse reference; donor-aware abundance models support an association rather than a causal mechanism. Cross-species label transfer, small donor cohorts and retrospective tissue limit generalization; inferred connectivity and candidate drug targets are not direct human tracing or demonstrated therapy.

Technology: Hierarchical mixture-of-experts classifier (logistic-regression experts)

Editorial relevance 53 biology Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A tree of trained logistic-regression experts predicts subtype labels from gene expression and transfers mouse taxonomy to human nuclei, enabling the subtype-specific disease comparison.
Test and result
Existing mouse/human transcriptomic data; 24,419 mouse reference cells, five-fold cell-stratified validation and a held-out test; human depletion comparison uses eight control and seven Parkinson’s donors with donor-aware mixed models.
Limitation / still unknown
Causal susceptibility, in-vivo human projections and therapeutic benefit are not established. Cells are not independent donor replicates; cross-species annotation may constrain inferred subtype identity.
Evidence sources and annotation context

23 Nov 2026University of Jyväskylä

MatterSim screening identifies low-conductivity lithium-metal candidates

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A preprint used the MatterSim learned interatomic potential to calculate phonons for 42,347 stable, previously observed compounds and flag 11,176 with predicted phonon gaps larger than 1 THz. Screening and existing-database thermal descriptors prioritized lithium-metal binaries; targeted first-principles Boltzmann calculations then predicted room-temperature lattice thermal conductivities of 2.47, 0.63 and 0.33 W m−1 K−1 for LiPt, LiTl and LiBi, respectively. These are computational properties of known compounds, not new synthesis or experimentally measured performance. The large candidate list is model-derived; a separate DFT benchmark does not independently validate every screened material.

Technology: MatterSim v1.0.0-5M machine-learning interatomic potential

Family MatterSim: profile · models page

Editorial relevance 60 materials Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The pretrained potential supplies relaxed structures and force responses for scalable phonon screening, enabling selection of specific low-thermal-conductivity lithium binaries.
Test and result
MatterSim phonon screening, separate 3,558-material DFT comparison and within-study first-principles third-order force-constant/Boltzmann calculations for LiPt, LiTl and LiBi. The latter check the specific low-conductivity candidate result computationally.
Limitation / still unknown
No new synthesis or experimental phonon/thermal-conductivity validation is reported. Model errors, dynamic-stability filtering and gap cutoffs affect candidate counts; not every candidate receives separate DFT confirmation.
Evidence sources and annotation context

Evidence context (descriptions follow the cited sources; test type is a catalog classification):

23 Nov 2026University of Texas at Austin; Flatiron Institute; Max Planck Institute of Biophysics

AlphaFold-guided tests link bacterial channel tails to temperature gating

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A preprint combined AlphaFold2-Multimer predictions, molecular dynamics and electrophysiology to study a sodium channel from the marine bacterium Ruegeria pomeroyi. Predicted tail conformations suggested contacts that stabilize channel opening and reduce temperature sensitivity; targeted substitutions altered temperature responses in the predicted contexts. The study argues that the tail modulates gating rather than serving as a temperature sensor itself. Predictions across more than 800 bacterial sequences suggest broader conservation, but those channels were not all experimentally tested. The result concerns bacterial-channel biophysics, not a demonstrated mechanism in human neurons.

Technology: AlphaFold2-Multimer; molecular dynamics and electrophysiological tests

Family AlphaFold: profile · models page

Editorial relevance 53 biology Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Learned multimer structure predictions identify tail conformations and candidate gating contacts that are checked experimentally.
Test and result
Within-study electrophysiology of predicted channel constructs and interface changes, supported by molecular dynamics.
Limitation / still unknown
Sequence-wide structural generalization is predicted; model confidence is not experimental confirmation of every bacterial channel or human-channel mechanism.
Evidence sources and annotation context

23 Nov 2026University of Sheffield; Zhejiang University of Technology; Harper Adams University; Velcourt Limited; Rothamsted Research

Grower-guided AI reduces nitrogen use in two wheat field pilots

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A preprint coupled TabPFN nitrogen-response recommendations with Gemini 3 Pro schedule adjustments reviewed by an agronomist. In 2025–26 commercial field trials at two sites, treatments used 24.1% and 12.5% less nitrogen than standard practice, with no statistically significant yield differences; four replicate plots per treatment and no equivalence test limit any claim of preserved yield. Earlier 2024–25 static recommendations lowered grain protein at all five sites, significantly at several; one in-season LLM error was caught by agronomist review. These small within-study pilots do not establish general farming benefit or reduced emissions.

Technology: TabPFN tabular foundation model; Gemini 3 Pro language model; agronomist-reviewed grower-in-the-loop scheduling

Family Gemini: profile · models page

Editorial relevance 60 climate Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
TabPFN predicts yield anchors for a nitrogen-response curve; Gemini 3 Pro proposes bounded schedule revisions from grower/weather constraints, each reviewed by an agronomist.
Test and result
Initial within-study field tests at two commercial sites in 2025–26, four plots per treatment; recommendations at sowing and agronomist-reviewed in-season adjustments preceded harvest measurements. Retrospective long-term model evaluations and earlier field seasons are ancillary evidence.
Limitation / still unknown
Nonsignificant yield differences are not equivalence. Sites were not replicated within season; wider transfer, grain-quality trade-offs and environmental benefit remain unestablished. Earlier measured N2O differences were nonsignificant.

23 Nov 2026University of Florida

Evolutionary optimization designs a simulated low-voltage photonic junction

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A preprint used NSGA-II evolutionary optimization to vary doping-mask geometry and jointly reduce modulation voltage–length product and optical loss in a silicon microring junction. The resulting C-shaped profile reached a simulated VπL of about 0.39 V·cm, compared with 0.44–0.72 V·cm for representative profiles listed by the authors. Carrier and optical simulations fed a four-bit photonic converter model. These are computational device-design results under stated process assumptions; no fabricated junction, measured bandwidth or demonstrated neural-network acceleration was reported.

Technology: NSGA-II multiobjective evolutionary optimization

Editorial relevance 47 physics Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Evolutionary search changes doping-mask geometry to minimize VπL and doping-induced optical loss. The downstream neural-network application is motivation, not evidence of AI operation.
Test and result
CHARGE carrier simulations and MODE optical calculations evaluate the NSGA-II-selected doping geometry; INTERCONNECT simulates the converter.
Limitation / still unknown
No fabrication or independent experimental validation; representative-profile comparisons are source-reported and process assumptions constrain transfer.
Evidence sources and annotation context

23 Nov 2026Jinan University; You Jiang Medical University for Nationalities

Learned lymphoma risk score shows retrospective associations and transport limits

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: CoxBoost plus a random survival forest produced a 16-gene DLBCL risk score after comparison of 101 model combinations. Reported validation C-indices were 0.613, 0.732 and 0.632, but those cohorts contributed to model selection. More strictly frozen preprocessing lowered discrimination to 0.547, 0.591 and 0.596; the training cutoff classified almost all external patients as high risk. Downstream transcriptomic analyses suggested a BAFF/APRIL pathway, with BAFF effects tested in cell lines. These findings establish neither clinical benefit nor a validated treatment mechanism, and the cell-line assays do not independently validate prognostic transport.

Technology: CoxBoost gene selection and random survival forest (CDIS)

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Published article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Learned gene selection and survival-forest predictions define CDIS for risk and immune-state comparisons.
Test and result
Existing-cohort survival scoring; three validation cohorts also informed selection. Separate cell-line BAFF assays concern a candidate pathway.
Limitation / still unknown
Prospective clinical benefit and portable preprocessing/cutoffs remain unestablished. Cell-line effects are not patient therapeutic evidence.

23 Nov 2026Beihang University; Qilu Hospital of Shandong University; Shandong First Medical University

MEG connectivity classifier shows limited lumbar-stenosis discrimination

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A preprint evaluated subject-level MEG connectivity classifiers in 39 lumbar-stenosis patients and 31 controls. Random forest achieved mean nested-cross-validation AUC 0.723 with fold standard deviation 0.262, indicating substantial uncertainty and variation. SHAP identified features contributing to model discrimination; independently tested physiological group differences are separate results, not all AI-discovered mechanisms. The small cross-sectional study provides neither external validation nor clinical diagnostic utility.

Technology: Random forest and nested group-aware MEG connectivity classification

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Learned classifiers map multi-band wPLI connectivity to case/control labels; SHAP characterizes model contributions.
Test and result
Nested group-aware internal cross-validation in 70 participants, keeping all data from each subject together and tuning inside training folds.
Limitation / still unknown
No external clinical performance or mechanism established; high fold variance and small cohort limit interpretation.

23 Nov 2026Guangzhou University of Chinese Medicine; Shanghai University of Traditional Chinese Medicine; Heilongjiang University of Chinese Medicine; Shanxi University of Chinese Medicine

TyG-BMI adds modest discrimination in retrospective cardiovascular prediction

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In 3,092 hypertensive CHARLS participants followed for a median eight years, a preprint compared learned cardiovascular-event predictors using baseline risk factors. Adding TyG-BMI increased reported AUC from 0.593 to 0.636; logistic regression performed best, with tree-based learners adding no advantage. This is retrospective modeling of recorded follow-up outcomes, not a prospective evaluation of a frozen model. Modest discrimination, self-reported outcomes and unresolved clinical utility limit application; the conventional epidemiological association is not itself a new AI-discovered mechanism.

Technology: Logistic regression, random forest, XGBoost, LightGBM and KNN

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Supervised models compare event discrimination with and without existing metabolic indices.
Test and result
Stratified 70/30 training/test split; preprocessing learned on training data, five-fold training hyperparameter tuning, recorded follow-up event labels.
Limitation / still unknown
Prospective clinical benefit and external transport unestablished; cohort follow-up does not make retrospective model evaluation prospective.

23 Nov 2026Ondokuz Mayıs University; Samsun University; Duke University School of Medicine; University of Massachusetts Amherst

Video gait features and clinical models estimate lung function in a small cohort

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A preprint used MediaPipe Pose to extract walking features and compared 13 regressors in 100 participants (33 classified with COPD). Five repeated 90/10 internal splits gave FEV1/FVC correlation 0.701±0.135 with gradient boosting and mid-expiratory-flow correlation 0.730±0.078 with histogram boosting; linear or regularized models were strongest for several volume targets. Test sets contained about 10 people and selecting models on the same splits can inflate performance. The study did not establish an incremental benefit from video features, external validity, replacement of spirometry or clinical benefit.

Technology: MediaPipe Pose; gradient boosting and histogram gradient boosting; supervised regression comparison

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Learned pose estimation supplies gait inputs; boosting regressors estimate selected spirometric targets. Ordinary linear models lead on several other targets.
Test and result
Prospectively collected single-centre cohort, but retrospective repeated internal train/test evaluation; no external validation.
Limitation / still unknown
Video contribution was not ablated against clinical-only models; small test sets and model selection on evaluation splits limit reliability. COPD classification used pre-bronchodilator spirometry.

23 Nov 2026Telethon Institute of Genetics and Medicine; University of Naples Federico II; Université Bourgogne Europe; University of Padua; Scuola Superiore Meridionale

Ensemble modeling explores OFD1 variant–phenotype associations retrospectively

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A preprint combined random forest, XGBoost, RBF-SVM and elastic-net learners to model OFD1 phenotype groups from variant features. The 356-case clinical compilation included 61 new cases, but prediction used a much smaller missense subset plus 39 benign variants assigned phenotype-absent labels; reported internal AUCs were 0.82–0.969. Model interpretation associated LisH-domain variants with higher predicted phenotype probabilities. These are retrospective model associations, not causal variant effects or demonstrated clinical utility. No independent OFD1 validation cohort was available; transfer to LMNA does not validate OFD1 predictions.

Technology: SuperLearner ensemble of random forest, XGBoost, RBF-SVM and elastic-net GLM

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Learned ensembles map variant conservation, predicted structural features and domain position to phenotype labels; SHAP describes model associations.
Test and result
Repeated internal cross-validation with nested tuning/evaluation; phenotype labels are retrospectively compiled and benign variants supply an absent-phenotype class.
Limitation / still unknown
External OFD1 performance, prospective utility and causal variant mechanisms remain unestablished; small missense subsets and literature ascertainment constrain interpretation.

23 Nov 2026Shanghai Pudong Hospital; Fudan University

CatBoost estimates six-month kidney-function category decline retrospectively

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A preprint evaluated baseline clinical prediction of six-month eGFR-category worsening in 2,022 adults with diabetes and renal dysfunction. CatBoost was selected within development data and reached AUROC 0.91 in a reserved 405-patient internal test set. Inputs were restricted to baseline and pre-index records. A post hoc top 5% prioritization rule identified19 of48 test-set events, missing more than half. Category worsening did not require 90-day persistence, so this is not confirmed chronic progression. The single-centre retrospective study establishes no external clinical utility or causal benefit from medication exposure.

Technology: CatBoost; training-fold XGBoost feature selection

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Gradient-boosted decision trees learn baseline features predicting a later eGFR-category label.
Test and result
Patient-level 80/20 split; fold-refitted preprocessing/feature selection and development-only 10-fold model selection; reserved internal test.
Limitation / still unknown
Single-centre retrospective inclusion depends on follow-up availability; post hoc threshold and medication scenarios are exploratory, not treatment effects.

23 Nov 2026University of Arizona; Ohio State University

Computer vision measures diverse tomato pollen responses to heat

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A preprint fine-tuned CenterNet HourGlass104 to count and track pollen in approximately 300,000 time-lapse images from 218 tomato accessions and wild relatives. In vitro, raising temperature from 26°C to 34°C increased mean bursting at two hours from 14.9% to 83.1%, while tube-length responses varied: 72% of accessions had shorter tubes and 28% had equal or greater length. Manual-count agreement supported image measurements. These are controlled pollen-assay phenotypes, not demonstrated field fertility or yield benefits; genotype effects and assay generalization need further testing.

23 Nov 2026University of Strathclyde

Linear decoding distinguishes sound conditions in aging mouse cortex

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A preprint reanalyzed auditory-cortex recordings and tested whether population activity distinguished silence, natural sounds and noise. In 33 mice with all three conditions recorded, a linear SVM achieved 82.50±8.74% mean accuracy using held-out time windows; template and linear-discriminant decoders gave similar results. Decoding remained above chance in both hearing-loss-prone and hearing-preserving strains across age groups, despite changes in individual-neuron activity. This does not establish intact perception, equivalence across ages or generalization to new mice; conditions were recorded in separate blocks.

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: For an enzyme studied in a plant natural-product pathway, the authors used an AlphaFold 3 structure model with substrate docking and molecular-dynamics analysis to nominate candidate pocket residues. Mutational tests found that replacing one predicted aromatic residue abolished measured activity, while a conservative aromatic substitution restored activity, supporting a role in substrate orientation. This is a bounded residue-level mechanistic result: the authors report that the mutations do not establish a comprehensive mechanism and the predicted protein and complex structures were not experimentally characterized.

Finding: A shared bidirectional LSTM with four regression heads predicted weight and dimensions from injection-molding sensor sequences. 20 Nov 2026Ludwig Maximilian University of Munich; University of Freiburg

MedNeXt localizes mandibular cancer invasion in a small retrospective CT cohort

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A single-center study trained a 3D MedNeXt model within nnU-Net on 30 CT examinations and evaluated it on 20 held-out patients with histopathologically confirmed mandibular invasion by oral squamous cell carcinoma. Predicted invasion overlapped expert-reviewed CT references in 18 of 20 examinations; mean invasion Dice was 0.553 and recall 0.483, with two cases missed. Among 18 evaluable cases, median absolute longitudinal invasion-span difference was 7.01 mm. This is a retrospective localization/segmentation study among invasion-positive patients, not an assessment of specificity, surgical outcomes or clinical benefit. Larger multicenter and prospective planning studies remain necessary.

20 Nov 2026University of Freiburg; Boston University; Loma Linda University

AI reconstructs maxillary denture bases from intraoral scans in a clinical feasibility study

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A PointNet++ model learned to reconstruct maxillary denture-base geometry from paired scans of 79 patients’ edentulous maxillae and existing dentures, using 57 training, seven validation and 15 test cases. Three randomly selected test outputs were manufactured and tried in patients; whole-surface registration RMSE ranged from 0.4 to 0.6 mm. Existing conventional dentures were subjectively more retentive than the newly printed AI bases. These descriptive, non-standardized try-ins do not establish clinical benefit, retention equivalence or border-specific reconstruction accuracy. Comparisons differed in material, manufacturing, prior wear and complete-denture versus base-only configuration. No objective retention or external validation was performed.

20 Nov 2026Renmin Hospital of Wuhan University; Wuhan University

Learned screening prioritizes FOS in human renal reperfusion data

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Researchers applied LASSO, random forest and support-vector-machine recursive feature elimination to renal transcriptomic data and converged on FOS and FOSB as candidates after kidney reperfusion. Both were elevated across 41 matched pre/post-reperfusion biopsy pairs, and an exploratory two-gene model reached an AUC of 0.922 on that discovery cohort; FOS alone performed similarly. In a separate dataset of 28 acute kidney injury biopsies and 11 stable controls, FOS remained elevated but FOSB did not. This is retrospective candidate evidence: the external dataset did not validate the combined diagnostic model, and the authors describe their analysis as exploratory rather than clinical model validation.

Technology: LASSO regression, random forest and support-vector-machine recursive feature elimination

Editorial relevance 40 medicine Published articlePublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
LASSO, random forest and SVM-RFE feature selection identified FOS and FOSB from renal transcriptomic candidates; FOS was the more reproducible injury-associated candidate in the separate AKI cohort.
Test and result
Three learned feature-selection approaches converged on FOS and FOSB in GSE126805, comprising 42 kidney-transplant recipients sampled at four time points; the paper excluded one unmatched post-reperfusion sample and analyzed 41 matched pre/post pairs. The exploratory two-gene ROC AUC of 0.922 and similar FOS-alone performance were estimated in this same discovery dataset, with no held-out evaluation reported. In the independent GSE30718 series (28 AKI biopsies and 11 stable controls), FOS remained elevated but FOSB did not. The authors explicitly characterize this as candidate-feature robustness assessment, not clinical validation of a diagnostic model.
Limitation / still unknown
The combined model was not independently evaluated; FOSB did not replicate in GSE30718, and FOS alone performed comparably in the discovery cohort. The study does not establish clinical utility, prospective performance, or a causal role for FOS/FOSB in lung-to-kidney signaling.

20 Nov 2026Maharshi Dayanand University

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A neural-network-assisted optimization selected a magnetic-nanoparticle formulation of arginine deiminase for citrulline production. The prepared catalyst achieved 70.0% activity recovery, near the predicted 71.18%, and 81.4% immobilization efficiency. It retained approximately 64% activity after 40 days at 4 °C, exceeded 74% after seven reuse cycles, and converted 99.1% of arginine to citrulline. These are laboratory formulation results; model training used only 30 observations and industrial or continuous-flow performance remains untested.

20 Nov 2026University of Antwerp; University of Liège; KU Leuven; Universidad Peruana Cayetano Heredia; Yale University

Elastic-net analysis finds limited genomic separation of malaria infections in two connected Amazonian communities

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: An exploratory preprint applied elastic-net logistic regression to existing genomic data from 47 Plasmodium vivax infections detected in two neighboring Peruvian Amazon communities, including 12 people reporting recent local travel. Repeated nested cross-validation yielded an AUC of 0.625 (95% bootstrap interval 0.522–0.724), versus 0.529 (0.402–0.657) after restricting the sample to non-travelers. This supports a bounded finding that fine-scale assignment was weak and sensitive to sample composition in this dataset. The analyses used different, overlapping infection sets, so their comparison does not isolate a causal effect of travel. Self-reported travel did not establish infection origin; intervals were conditional on saved predictions, and no independent cohort or operational surveillance benefit was tested.

A finger-cuff waveform CNN screens for aortic stenosis in a multicenter cohort

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A one-dimensional convolutional model learned to identify at least moderate aortic stenosis from noninvasive finger-cuff arterial pressure waveforms, using echocardiography as reference. Full manuscript Methods, prespecification and locked-model timing not assessed. Low positive predictive value implies confirmatory testing remains relevant; outcome benefit and deployment effectiveness not established.

20 Nov 2026University of Cambridge; Durham University; Stanford University

Machine-learned density functional theory reveals molecular control of electrolyte screening

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Machine-learned neural classical density functional theory trained on grand-canonical Monte Carlo data enabled calculations of asymptotic screening behavior across increasingly molecularly detailed electrolyte models. The authors report that solvent attraction creates a monotonic decay mode, solvent polarity produces re-entrant behavior, and ionic architecture changes screening-length exponents, challenging a single universal scaling law. These are computational predictions for modelled systems; the reviewed paper reports no experimental validation of the new predictions.

Finding: A single-center cross-sectional study of 102 inpatients with major depression and 38 controls used machine-learning feature selection on gut-microbiome profiles to identify nine biologically annotated microbial modules. 20 Nov 2026Amirkabir University of Technology

Bayesian modeling estimates configuration-dependent uncertainty in printed PLA bending

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A Research Square preprint trained a Bayesian physics-informed neural network on bending data from 60 groups of printed PLA specimens and combined it with finite-element simulation. In ten within-study validation configurations, predicted uncertainty varied by raster angle and infill, with the 60-degree and reduced-infill configurations exceeding twice the estimated variation of the 0-degree configuration. All ten experimental validation values lay within the reported 95% intervals, but the sample is too small to establish precise uncertainty calibration. The result is limited to the studied printer, material and bending conditions, without separate-team replication or demonstrated service reliability.

Technology: Bayesian physics-informed neural network with Monte Carlo dropout embedded in finite-element analysis

Editorial relevance 40 materials PreprintScientific article Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A physics-constrained neural network with Monte Carlo dropout estimates flexural-modulus distributions; sampled moduli are propagated through finite-element simulations to estimate configuration-dependent prediction uncertainty.
Test and result
The PINN was developed from experimental data for 60 FDM polylactic-acid specimen groups, with ten independent configurations evaluated. Reported R² was 0.9935, MAPE 1.85%, and 95% confidence intervals contained all ten experimental validation values. Reliability analysis estimated coefficient-of-variation uncertainty of 3.8–9.0% across configurations and corresponding safety factors of 1.11–1.30.
Limitation / still unknown
The ten configurations are within-study validation, not a separate-team replication. Sample size is limited; the results do not establish transfer to other printers, materials, component geometries, or load/service conditions.

20 Nov 2026Loudi Central Hospital

Cohort audit finds unstable performance in efferocytosis-related prognostic scores

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A retrospective audit rebuilt an efferocytosis-related head-and-neck-cancer prognostic analysis in 519 primary tumors after detecting non-primary rows in the original cohort. A Cox-LASSO workflow selected a nine-gene sensitivity score, but its exact composition recurred in none of 500 full-pipeline bootstrap samples and repeated nested cross-validation yielded a mean C-index of 0.587. Survival associations persisted in some analyses while external performance was inconsistent, and a fixed cutoff did not transfer. The authors treat both this reconstruction and the historical six-gene score as hypotheses, with overlapping prior cohorts, no demonstrated causal mechanism and no established clinical utility.

20 Nov 2026Freie Universität Berlin; Technical University of Denmark

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Analysis of structural models for 56 C-active UGT enzymes found acceptor gates throughout the set and two enzymes lacking a discernible donor gate; AlphaFold3 and source-labeled RobettaFold models supported variable open/closed gate conformations. 20 Nov 2026The University of Texas MD Anderson Cancer Center; Mayo Clinic

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In a prospectively collected paired feasibility series of seven patients, Sonopet aspirate and standard-resection skull-base meningioma specimens produced comparable sequencing yield, read quality and tumor purity. Copy-number profiles were highly concordant (Lin’s CCC0.99;95% CI0.76–1.00), whereas somatic variant-burden agreement was weaker and imprecise (CCC0.53;95% CI−0.28–0.99); promoter methylation was closely correlated. 20 Nov 2026City University Malaysia

Vocabulary app with Qwen tutoring shows a short-term score advantage in eight classes

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A twelve-week teaching study randomized four matched pairs of university tutorial classes to a vocabulary application or non-adaptive word lists. The application bundled Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct examples, wav2vec2.0 pronunciation feedback and rule-based review scheduling. Among 92 completers from 100 allocated students, class-adjusted posttest scores favored the application by 6.6 points on a 100-point test (95% CI 1.3–11.9). This is a prospective comparison of the complete instructional package, not an isolated effect of AI. Eight classes, repeated test items, complete-case analysis and no delayed-retention measure limit generalization.

20 Nov 2026University of Electronic Science and Technology of China; City University of Hong Kong; The University of Tokyo

Learned wing sensing supports damage detection and lift recovery in a flapping robot

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A prototype flapping-wing robot used integrated wing sensors with a learned Kriging surrogate to reconstruct wing motion and a linear SVM to classify damaged states. The SVM achieved 94.6% accuracy on 168 held-out samples, and a separate damaged-wing bench demonstration increased flapping frequency within 0.1 seconds to restore average lift near the intact baseline. These are device-specific physical sensing and control results, with calibration and comparison against binocular-vision measurements. They do not establish autonomous free-flight performance, generalization across robot designs or sustained field operation.

Technology: Physics-informed Kriging surrogate model and linear support vector machine (SVM)

Editorial relevance 53 other Published articlePublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The physics-informed Kriging surrogate maps measured voltage/current amplitudes and flapping frequency to wing-motion angles; a linear SVM classifies faults from the derived signal features. These learned operations support the experimentally demonstrated intrinsic motion sensing, fault classification and lift-compensation control.
Test and result
The Kriging model was calibrated using two signal cycles and evaluated on other cycles at each frequency against binocular-vision measurements; reported mean unit-pitching-angle error was below 10% and maximum flapping-angle error 1.1%. For fault diagnosis, 504 samples trained a linear SVM and 168 test samples yielded 94.6% accuracy across normal and three failure states. The authors also report a damaged-wing feedback demonstration with frequency rising from 12.9 to 19.3 Hz in 0.1 seconds and average lift restored near the intact baseline.
Limitation / still unknown
Generalization to other wing structures, sensor builds, operating conditions, and untethered flight; evidence for field deployment or sustained operational use.

19 Nov 2026Fabrice Baudoin

GPT-6.1 Astra assists a gradient rank theorem for metric measure spaces

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Fabrice Baudoin develops a finite-rank gradient framework and proves that the pointwise gradient rank is strictly below p almost everywhere under the hypotheses of his rank theorem. The result assumes a small-scale doubling measure, a Poincaré inequality and cutoffs with a matching energy scale above the exponent p.

19 Nov 2026Stanford University

Calibrated AI detectors estimate rising AI-assisted drafting in four state courts

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Stanford researchers applied the learned EditLens detector with ScoreMix calibration to 127,518 complaints from four U.S. county court systems. Their selected settings estimated rising AI-like drafting, reaching about 38% in Santa Clara and 36% in San Francisco in 2026 for non-debt-collection complaints. Calibration used documented hallucination filings and assumed their detector-score distribution represented other AI-assisted writing. Detector settings, imperfect controls, case mix and selection of conspicuous AI mistakes limit the estimates. These are population estimates, not proof that any particular filing used AI, fabricated citations or affected a judicial outcome. November 19 is the institutional announcement date; the working paper posting day is unestablished.

Technology: EditLens (Llama-3.2-3B); ScoreMix calibration

16 Nov 2026Washington University in St. Louis

QORA designs quantum control sequences that extend spin coherence in diamond

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In an arXiv preprint, Washington University in St. Louis researchers report that QORA designed AID-ROD-1, a sequence that nearly doubled measured coherence time relative to DROID-C3PO in a dense ensemble of spin-1 nitrogen-vacancy centers in diamond. The agent system used simulation and laboratory feedback under human oversight. The research team subsequently analyzed the mechanisms behind the improvement. The comparison is specific to the tested ensemble, and independent replication was not established in the reviewed preprint.

Technology: QORA (GPT-6 Astra and Sorol)

16 Nov 2026National Institute of Advanced Industrial Science and Technology; Nagaoka University of Technology

Breaking the Fixed-Substrate Limitation of Deep Learning Ellipsometry with Pseudo-Substrate Spectra

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding / tested result: FiLM-DLE takes both a thin-film spectrum and a pre-deposition pseudo-substrate spectrum as inputs. On 47 measured films across diverse substrates, its optical-constant and thickness estimates had mean absolute errors of 0.047, 0.030 and 1.52 nm, respectively, against manual analysis; each analysis took under two seconds.

Technology: Two-input deep-learning ellipsometry using pseudo-substrate spectra
